| Composable Soft-Prompt Distillation of RL Experts: Toward Continual Post-Training | ★★★★☆ | [2611.27376](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27376) | Zhen Zhang et al. | 2026-11-20 | 2026-11-27 | Composable Soft-Prompt Distillation (CSPD) keeps the pre-RL base model frozen and distills each RL expert independently into a small trainable soft prompt; experts compose by concatenating prompts, so capabilities can be added or removed without retraining existing experts or re-consolidating the model. A single soft prompt recovers at least 90% of the teacher's gain in 22 of 23 math, code, and agent settings; the full multi-expert composition matches multi-teacher on-policy distillation (67.83 vs 67.79) and generalizes best across six held-out benchmarks (56.6 vs 53.5 for MOPD). Input-space adaptation keeps throughput within 1.5% of the backbone and reaches 2.40x rank-4 LoRA throughput with 32 concurrent adapters. UCSB-AI; code released. |

A Qwen3-4B student distilled from an RL-trained Qwen3-4B teacher beats the teacher on math (66.38 vs 64.16 average across 8 benchmarks, vs 64.71 for plain on-policy distillation); simply adding GRPO to OPD barely moves it (64.75). Strong-to-weak (4B teacher to Qwen3-1.7B): 53.32 vs 50.88 OPD. Math only; code released. |
